## TCGPMD.

<image-desc>The image shows the front cover of a 17th-century leather-bound book. The brown leather is decorated with blind-stamped designs. At the top, the initials "TCGPMD." are stamped in large capital letters. In the center is a large oval containing a coat of arms. The shield is divided into four quarters: the first and fourth quarters feature three fleurs-de-lis, while the second and third quarters have a checkered (chequy) pattern. The shield is surmounted by a helmet with elaborate scrolling mantling and a crest featuring a six-pointed star between two outspread wings. At the bottom of the cover, the year "1 6 7 6 ." is stamped in large, widely spaced digits. The entire composition is framed by a rectangular border of thin lines with floral ornamental stamps in the corners and at the center of each side.</image-desc>

## 1 6 7 6 .

<vocab>binding, supralibros, coat of arms, heraldry, chequy, fleur-de-lis, 17th century</vocab>

<page-type>frontispiece</page-type>
<meta>This page features a hand-colored printed emblem pasted onto a flyleaf or title page, accompanied by manuscript notes in Latin.</meta>

Lib. b. §. 27. 40. 52. inter sphaeras Somniorum

<insert>A</insert>

->**PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA**<-

<image-desc>
A circular/oval emblem titled "PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA" in golden letters. The central icon is framed by an Ouroboros (a green serpent devouring its own tail). The background is a blue oval field. At the top of the field is a golden sun with radiating rays. In the center, a white pelican "in its piety" plucks its breast to feed its young with its own blood (a traditional alchemical symbol for the Great Work). The pelican perches upon a white crescent moon, which is situated atop a white cube or pedestal. The front face of the cube displays a saltire cross with four red roses in its quadrants.
</image-desc>

<vocab>Philosophia Hermetica, Ouroboros, Pelican in its Piety, Saltire, Alchemy, Hermeticism, Somniorum</vocab>

<margin>92</margin> *Virtus rosarum purgatrix consistit in supernis earum partibus, ut etiam violarum: adstrictoria vero earūdem facultas & roboris altrix, in internis delitescit. Sic urticæ vis adurens superficie tenus manet: interne verò tantum frigoris latet, ut contusa urtica, & arterijs brachij temporūmque applicata, febris feruorem extinguat in corde, ut alibi adnotauimus.*

<margin>93</margin> *Regum pocula ex cornu olim fuisse, autor est Philostratus. Vnde ἀπὸ τοῦ κέρατος, id est, à cornu, cratera deduci contendunt. Tradit insuper Theopompus, boues apud Pæonas tam magna gestâsse cornua, ut tribus, aut quatuor vini sextarijs vix adimplerētur: ex ijsque reges ac proceres pocula conficerent, labris eorū auro & argento obductis.*

<margin>94</margin> *Ad maculas facieiobliterandas plurimùm pollet lupinorum farina.*
